### Random input weights can generate Gaussian maps

Location: line 242, anchor `sec:PLACE-WIRING`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 244
Closing passage line: 351

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p244 sha256 30e3871f3bad882f606126c235a5966435172ad279aeb11adc8329e5945ee265 -->
p244, lines 244-244; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p239; next: p246

> The statistical model becomes biologically useful if it suggests how a circuit could make the maps. A random weighted sum of many inputs is one possible mechanism, and the supplement makes its assumptions explicit.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p246 sha256 b9e8a01a99c81ad40a40d1bd24984b697614e6eb71a3034df68479569c3f916c -->
p246, lines 246-246; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p244; next: p248

> At position $x$, each input cell contributes according to its spatial firing profile. Fixed connection weights determine how those contributions combine at the receiving cell. The source treats the sum as effective input relative to a background that can be balanced through inhibition or regulation of excitation and inhibition. The signed random weights below are part of this simplified input model. They do not establish the signs of individual biological synapses.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p248 sha256 e1f790cefb655302fbd37c34a3be0616c73966e518fa76fad4af5f09b43a2753 -->
p248, lines 248-248; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p246; next: p254

> Let $u_i(x)$ be the fixed spatial tuning of input neuron $i$, and let independent weights $W_i$ have zero mean and variance $v_W$. The profiles $u_i$ may have nonzero spatial means. The expectation below is over weights: their zero means make the summed input $h(x)$ have mean zero. The summed input is

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p254 sha256 8b81b66d4d14a0ac3df10392c30954cb1af30182ad0c559397e9a3c98f279359 -->
p254, lines 254-256; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p248; next: p258

> <!-- vale ai-tells.FillerIntensifier = NO --> [At a finite list of positions this is a sum of independent random vectors, one per input cell. Under a multivariate central limit condition, with a suitable normalization and no single input dominating, the sum approaches a Gaussian vector as the number of contributing inputs grows. Applying this at every finite list supplies the Gaussian-process description. This Gaussian limit requires many contributing inputs at each position. A large anatomical input count alone does not establish how many inputs contribute there.]{.key-statement}[^p3-wiring-source] <!-- vale ai-tells.FillerIntensifier = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p258 sha256 d2a002d6434381ff1dc22fa84811a52d1e42a1ec251760afaadf1cda16ded894 -->
p258, lines 258-258; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p254; next: p268

> The covariance calculation exposes which assumption supplies spatial structure:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p268 sha256 f064a7ad714e4230814a9c94608a1cdbb1fc40debcd743cd2e5bf9201454865f -->
p268, lines 268-270; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p258; next: p272

> <!-- vale ai-tells.FormalTransitions = NO --> The same fixed weight multiplies an input cell's activity at both positions. For positive input profiles, cells active at both $x$ and $y$ therefore make positive contributions to covariance across possible weight choices. Little overlap gives a smaller contribution. For profiles defined as deviations from background, the products can also be negative. Broad input profiles can produce broad spatial covariance, as the Gaussian example below shows. <!-- vale ai-tells.FormalTransitions = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p272 sha256 597d32eb83bc21fe4c797636463d0e5fd28d6b21905a2fad133f199afeef9d36 -->
p272, lines 272-272; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p268; next: p278

> If inputs are translated copies $u_i(x)=u_0(x-x_i)$ with centres densely and uniformly covering space at density $\rho$, the sum is approximately

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p278 sha256 ebb579e0fc866a1f90ab68176d44e64d032b011e4debdfddd14b2dc7fc6e5a13 -->
p278, lines 278-278; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p272; next: p280

> The assumptions supply different parts of the model:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p280 sha256 90c27326ab42b5537c52010bf559cd2ff3643ffbab33375d768e984eb660729a -->
p280, lines 280-280; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p278; next: p281

> Uniform coverage supplies stationarity away from boundaries.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p281 sha256 7153af7d68d9a6d40b69b03df0baf14d95b7ab089d10a1e4f6355679a6e62844 -->
p281, lines 281-281; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p280; next: p282

> Smooth input tuning supplies smoothness.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p282 sha256 d6d431ae6dbbfd92402270596e783b84f6b03e75903f9e87c6c59e7dc9d32d0b -->
p282, lines 282-282; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p281; next: p284

> Random weights supply the Gaussian limit under their independence and size conditions.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p284 sha256 d05f1c44bcdc894deae5496fcd9bdf4608fd0bb389533b73165de5bef5865130 -->
p284, lines 284-284; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p282; next: p286

> For input profiles with different shapes, average the last integral over the distribution of those shapes, as in Methods S2, Equation S8.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p286 sha256 1802ca0bd310d2485430f44358eec676a43663fac05d55c5c9bec5a38d82b9b8 -->
p286, lines 286-286; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p284; next: p288

> At fixed profile shape, increasing the weight variance $v_W$ or input density $\rho$ increases the covariance amplitude. It does not change the normalized spatial dependence in this construction. Widening each input profile instead broadens the distances over which positions share input. These are different changes: input amplitude can alter the effective threshold $q$ when the absolute threshold is held fixed, whereas input width changes the spatial scale.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p288 sha256 cdc537748d6682689ae461fffa2b4fc3db232c9eb7355a9362b24eac1d7b8765 -->
p288, lines 288-290; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p286; next: p297

> <!-- vale ai-tells.FormalTransitions = NO --> For example, take $u_0(s)=R_0e^{-s^2/(2b^2)}$ in one dimension. Completing the square gives <!-- vale ai-tells.FormalTransitions = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p297 sha256 98b28bbeb7835ea3e4c956ee74d5e7ca58b3c878e8246b85f50b22fdc3763114 -->
p297, lines 297-297; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p288; next: p299

> The covariance's Gaussian standard deviation is $\sqrt2 b$, fixing the width convention used in the comparison.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p299 sha256 5e2218b085204b0b3f4a2be321f3d313672f92417579901b0406759f1939b6e0 -->
p299, lines 299-301; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p297; next: p303

> <!-- vale ai-tells.StrawmanContrast = NO --> Agreement with the Gaussian field predictions supports this random-summation account as a possible explanation. It does not uniquely recover synaptic weights from rate maps. Correlated inputs, a structured component added to many random contributions, plasticity, or recurrent processing could also produce approximately Gaussian spatial input under some conditions. The paper measures rate maps, rather than the relevant weights or subthreshold input directly. Its analyzed environments were familiar and fairly uniform. Stationarity may fail near landmarks, boundaries, rewards, or in changing tasks.[^p3-discussion] <!-- vale ai-tells.StrawmanContrast = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p303 sha256 d8a058a32c40f249f8209c60e4bbee99dc3abb471cb5e7bd359837a5fa512b05 -->
p303, lines 303-305; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p299; next: p307

> <!-- vale ai-tells.NounString = NO --> [Thresholding a smooth spatial Gaussian input predicts field arrangement, shape, and topology from a small set of parameters. Several comparisons with recordings support these predictions. Inferring a circuit mechanism from this agreement requires additional measurements or perturbations.]{.key-statement} <!-- vale ai-tells.NounString = YES -->

## ch:SYNTHESIS - Spatial maps and memory dynamics need separate tests

Source file: `content/chapters/06-synthesis.md`

Purpose: The three papers address different links between nerve-cell activity and behavior: retaining a value, producing eye movement, and representing location. This chapter compares what the models and recordings establish, and separates supported connections from further questions about how a complete neural circuit would work.

Prerequisites: ch:MEMORY-READOUT, ch:EYE-CIRCUIT, ch:PLACE-TESTS

Sidecar sections: The three papers constrain maps, dynamics, and movement, A shared position error changes rates according to their tuning slopes, Example: opposite rate changes from the same position error, Each model needs a test matched to its claim, Related studies change assumptions and leave circuit choices open, A joint test would compare tuning maps with population fluctuations

Blocks: 1 figure(s), 0 equation(s), 0 code block(s)

Result labels: No Answer/Conclusion/Feedback label detected

> [A model can explain the shape of a neural code without explaining how that code is maintained. It can also explain fluctuations inside a circuit without uniquely locating the source of a measured behavior. Comparing the three papers requires keeping each claim with the mathematical or experimental test that supports it.]{.key-statement}

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p5 sha256 886128c3ee7f140661d1da9e1d92591356e55ef5512e9f5b0fc8a286ee263506 -->

> <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = NO --> To connect them, we first separate the spatial map, the changing represented position, and the movement measured downstream. A short calculation then shows how a common position error can make cells with different tuning change together. Comparing that calculation with each paper's evidence and related studies identifies what a joint experiment would still need to measure. <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = YES -->

### The three papers constrain maps, dynamics, and movement

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 11
Closing passage line: 29

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p11 sha256 4ddff5cc89973337f281a21893926b95e80419eaa76d94e566e5c4cf7b6e02e2 -->
p11, lines 11-11; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p9; next: p13

> <!-- vale ai-tells.CataphoricForecasting = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p13 sha256 0b80f24a27e581a9c2f5e86fe66ccc8e2ca7aaf40b5983a4a102100b07388aff -->
p13, lines 13-13; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p11; next: p15

> For a circuit representing position, three questions arise:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p15 sha256 a9a4d7fa3d020076c9f6f82d4de4ee3f54b1dd07ac0833e9a89b6b2bc55a7da3 -->
p15, lines 15-15; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p13; next: p16

> **Spatial map:** What activity pattern corresponds to each position? The place-field model addresses this question.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p16 sha256 1ab2eef073fbfc193d9f2724f85d531095153a9491777e56d38a4f564305f00f -->
p16, lines 16-16; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p15; next: p17

> **Memory motion:** How does the represented position evolve when there is no corrective input? The persistent-activity model addresses this question.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p17 sha256 3c8bc1157fe708f7af2b8d416c696bb1cb5b548a00af076d59a022f20713d336 -->
p17, lines 17-17; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p16; next: p19

> **Observed movement:** How does a downstream physical system transform that changing signal into an observable movement? The eye-drift model combines a candidate account of memory motion with a detailed account of this transformation.[^s-three]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p19 sha256 5f0d743f2d1f49e3a96956d00be8a1bf6f9a70134b740a7f87c145add2891d5f -->
p19, lines 19-19; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p17; next: p21

> The objects differ even when the word position appears in all three. In 2012, $\theta(t)$ locates a network state on its attractor. In 2022, the internal eye-position command and actual eye angle are separate quantities linked through motor neurons and mechanics. In 2025, $x$ locates an animal in an environment, and $h_i(x)$ is a spatial input function assigned to a cell. The Gaussian process does not update a remembered $x$ through time.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p21 sha256 3bce5cfb23a3680a57742cebd05e7ee506a21490d771b6e569e19be03db625af -->
p21, lines 21-21; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p19; next: p23

> The [guide map](ch-persistence.html#fig-foundations-paper-map) gives a visual comparison of these three questions.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p23 sha256 9960784fd6b96a12fbf5b8855619bf1a26dae079bce219c3b96e0c64ec075ad2 -->
p23, lines 23-23; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p21; next: p27

> <div class="table-responsive">

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p27 sha256 aac32651b10f567c461b9b4f255d6fb1fa6859b5368d8bd9a51af920ab21cf23 -->
p27, lines 27-27; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p23; next: p29

> </div>

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p29 sha256 37ffdd708ba8474c6b1445eee3bb7c1ed842b4c97da9f56fa84d04086d56644c -->
p29, lines 29-29; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p27; next: p33

> The 2012 and 2022 connection is direct: neural noise can move a maintained eye-position command, which then drives muscles. The 2022 result provides evidence for a substantial shared source before the final motor neurons and shows that integrator diffusion is sufficient within the constructed model. It does not measure the circuit's Fisher information or test equality in the 2012 bound. The place-field model describes spatially irregular tuning curves. To connect those curves to memory dynamics, we must also specify how the represented position moves.

<!-- pedagogy-unit: content/chapters/06-synthesis.md::sec:SYNTHESIS-EVIDENCE -->

### Each model needs a test matched to its claim

Location: line 101, anchor `sec:SYNTHESIS-EVIDENCE`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 103
Closing passage line: 115

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p103 sha256 bf26aa5ad95f5aa3bc6f807fedf818b62a3e2c4b2b3ae9dfd59c6b4ec7d43255 -->
p103, lines 103-103; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p95; next: p105

> The shared-position calculation predicts how rates would change together. Establishing that connection requires measurements of those changes as well as the spatial maps. Each paper tests a different part of the proposed connection:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p105 sha256 fb60ad9a9bcb5e02812b4e436b52511fc0f6cbe99bc1a64356307ad29b25afa2 -->
p105, lines 105-105; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p103; next: p107

> **Place-field statistics:** A model that matches one distribution may have enough adjustable parameters to do so for several different reasons. The place-field paper gains support by fitting arrangement parameters and then comparing additional features, including slices and shape statistics. The separate firing-rate multiplier used in some comparisons should remain visible when counting how many quantities were predicted without further fitting. A successful high-threshold formula is also a statement about its limiting assumptions. Finite-threshold simulations and the original recordings provide different kinds of checks.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p107 sha256 fd78940b37794bd6dde27714c3d8135160d4cd46030584b28e2f3603ac2eb838 -->
p107, lines 107-108; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p105; next: p109

> <!-- vale ai-tells.FigurativeCarries = NO --> <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = NO -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p109 sha256 675e0a805c9273afd2f3d8c054157aed5b96a24cc30fe5da9709f6d2f8ae60db -->
p109, lines 109-111; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p107; next: p113

> **Eye drift:** The eye paper calibrates a movement-to-firing relationship, inverts it, and compares the resulting single-neuron displacement predictions with measured displacement. Shared covariance then carries information that independent private fluctuations cannot supply under the decomposition's assumptions. The additional MSD comparison tests the time structure: increasing stationary motor-noise amplitude cannot by itself reproduce the accumulating upstream signal. Calibration validity, source correlations, two animals, and horizontal movement remain part of the result's scope. <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = YES --> <!-- vale ai-tells.FigurativeCarries = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p113 sha256 eaed7347e4b039b5f31c72c0e094ca779ba83bd4563f6a7b4780fba605acbed2 -->
p113, lines 113-113; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p109; next: p114

> <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = NO -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p114 sha256 5bb13c0ddc7ce9d940d1f53a2f469f00139f75cadb9519db30c6cd4063b471c1 -->
p114, lines 114-115; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p113; next: p119

> **Memory stability:** The 2012 inequality is a mathematical constraint rather than an experimental fit. Its derivation specifies what noise and dynamics it covers. An experimental comparison would need estimates of diffusion, the relevant time constants, and the appropriate instantaneous information rate, all under compatible conditions. Agreement with $1/N$ scaling alone would not test the entire inequality, since several mechanisms can share that scaling. <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-unit: content/chapters/06-synthesis.md::sec:SYNTHESIS-RELATED -->

### Related studies change assumptions and leave circuit choices open

Location: line 117, anchor `sec:SYNTHESIS-RELATED`

First content kind: `prose`

Opening passage line: 119
Closing passage line: 129

Paragraphs in source order (complete `PARAGRAPH_REVIEW.json`):

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p119 sha256 2fa4f402fb3c9140a5e2737da457f19171d7f49686a14d7e5e2060ddbf538125 -->
p119, lines 119-119; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p114; next: p121

> Related studies test the assumptions and ask questions the three papers leave open:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p121 sha256 9fb495d78d188dee3a9a3498ca426138e21b7a64f2850e2231757468224f9ccb -->
p121, lines 121-121; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p119; next: p123

> **Integrating movement:** The eye-position account begins with Seung's line-attractor model. Aksay and colleagues then used recordings and perturbations in goldfish to constrain integrator circuitry. These establish the dynamical problem and experimental architecture behind the 2022 model. Neither supplies the macaque displacement-variance estimate. Burak and Fiete's earlier grid-cell study examines [[**path integration**]{.definition-term custom-style="Definition Term"}, updating a position estimate from movement.]{.definition-explanation} In its model, velocity drives recurrent population activity, and errors accumulate in the position estimate. Its accuracy ranges belong to the simulated networks and parameter choices, rather than all biological grid circuits.[^s-attractors]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p123 sha256 b6f84f89cbbe52107ccd79515ab1f664b74c1d01dcbfd06ea57452c06d15e314 -->
p123, lines 123-123; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p121; next: p125

> **Storage capacity and changing synapses:** Noise-driven storage also connects to memory capacity. Koyluoglu and colleagues ask how noise limits recall as the delay and number of stored items increase. Their comparison with human data shows why direct storage of several independent values need not capture observed behavior. Transformations before and after storage alter the performance prediction. Seeholzer and colleagues examine how short-term changes in synaptic strength affect diffusion and systematic drift. These papers change the storage assumptions rather than invalidate the 2012 calculation inside its original model.[^s-extensions]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p125 sha256 35ecf69af1e00f263011e694a4578723a886e6b09ba2380aba18ba9c4469a0de -->
p125, lines 125-125; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p123; next: p127

> **Recordings from larger environments:** For place fields, Eliav and colleagues' long-tunnel recordings and Harland and colleagues' large-arena recordings supply the multiple-field, multiple-scale observations. Yartsev and Ulanovsky supply the three-dimensional bat recordings. The 2025 paper reanalyzes those existing data, so its agreement across dimensions is not three newly collected experiments under one protocol. The later study by Maimon and colleagues directly compares CA3 and CA1 in large tunnels: predominantly single fields in CA3 contrast with multiple fields in CA1, while field sizes are similar. That distinction supports treating the CA1 statistics and the upstream spatial circuit as related but separately constrained parts of the explanation.[^s-place-data]

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p127 sha256 b3e48d140901ca9ab3abcefe4ee25e6605cc7547a86ccfee0f978fdb96967f17 -->
p127, lines 127-127; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p125; next: p128

> <!-- vale ai-tells.FormalTransitions = NO -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p128 sha256 16bca599614c06567d2abd58128908eccd3d3bf8b270e1b2facf5044abbcba91 -->
p128, lines 128-129; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p127; next: p131

> **Connectivity and learning:** Gaussian spatial statistics do not identify a unique pattern of connections. Druckmann and colleagues report structured synaptic connectivity between hippocampal regions. Bittner and colleagues show how electrical events in [[**dendrite**]{.definition-term custom-style="Definition Term"}s, the branching input structures of neurons,]{.definition-explanation} can induce changes in synaptic strength and form CA1 place fields. Structure and learning can therefore be present even when a random-function model describes many aggregate field statistics. The model's success leaves open which circuit mechanism produces these maps. Input, connectivity, learning, or perturbation measurements could distinguish the possibilities.[^s-wiring] <!-- vale ai-tells.FormalTransitions = YES -->

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p131 sha256 0ff5e5f6c91d8070a3687305852c8017a4c42373f6fa5489195fa9f6d61ba483 -->
p131, lines 131-131; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p128; next: p135

> **Benefits for perception:** The source and use of eye motion are different questions. Nghiem and colleagues combine human eye tracking with a model of how eye movement affects visual signals to explain why an intermediate amount of fixational motion can help fine visual discrimination. A movement that partly originates from internal noise can still have useful sensory consequences, and feedback can regulate its statistics. Benefit for perception does not uniquely identify the neural generator.[^s-acuity]


Claim-bearing content: none

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p135 sha256 b24788061e16be10b5024058afee1e1e0efb572d33bf5c409f8b22b85e6775da -->
p135, lines 135-135; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p131; next: p137

> [A joint test needs tuning maps and fluctuations from the same population.]{.key-statement} A possible sequence is:

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p137 sha256 dcf643870dff7a8c4d647743e1e653d482f30cc97b7b4b18c08365b0c2bfe28e -->
p137, lines 137-137; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p135; next: p138

> Estimate the tuning maps and their uncertainty.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p138 sha256 8abed503bea87e5f7ca0fa95cefe7acf4ad69e22af38581e2701b568c035b4ca -->
p138, lines 138-138; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p137; next: p139

> Measure fluctuations when external signals provide little correction of the represented position. Test whether their covariance aligns with the changes implied by the tuning maps.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p139 sha256 ba5c6c1493d5d94d8f7d9010d5ef5bdd1e6bb4a4c6f7a38da8f695dcf749f75f -->
p139, lines 139-139; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p138; next: p140

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p140 sha256 7fb58465a9c8a72b3907619e2e542823ad8d388d2c66ccdd3badfce559784de2 -->
p140, lines 140-140; list_item; priority: unchanged; previous: p139; next: p142

> **If comparing behavior,** include the physical or neural filters between the representation and the measurement.

<!-- pedagogy-paragraph: p142 sha256 4385edf93d9588331b5978acd51537f3bdc7ea0e354de0850cca1176192ba9b1 -->
p142, lines 142-144; prose; priority: unchanged; previous: p140; next: p146

> <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = NO --> These measurements would test whether one system satisfies both spatial-statistics and dynamical-stability constraints. [A static Gaussian-process fit alone cannot supply the diffusion coefficient. A diffusion fit alone cannot establish the distribution of fields.]{.key-statement} Agreement with both would constrain the model more than either fit alone. A mismatch would require further tests of spatial input statistics, recurrent maintenance, the observation filter, or their coupling. This is proposed follow-up work, not a replication claimed by this guide. <!-- vale ai-tells.VerbTricolon = YES -->
